足許の経済・金融状況と注目ポイント

2026. 11.2
先週（10/26～10/30）発表の主な経済指標
AI関連を中心に設備投資が増勢を維持し、産業用機械にも広
がり。好調な企業収益も投資を後押し。個人消費も堅調。金
融所得や移転所得、株高による資産効果が購買力を下支え
政策要因を除く都区部CPIは上昇基調。財価格の上昇が目立
つ一方、サービス価格改定の広がりは限定的。企業の価格転嫁
は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが値上げの重荷に
注目点② 日本で進む価格転嫁、節約志向との綱引きが続く注目点①米国経済は投資の裾野拡大と消費の強さを確認
7～9月期GDPは成⾧加速。AI関連を中心とする設
備投資の拡大に加え、個人消費も堅調推移。9月
のコアPCE物価は3％を超える伸びが継続。景気の
底堅さを背景にFRBにもう一段の金融引き締め余地

労働分配率は低下傾向。賃金
以外の所得が家計所得を補完
米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完
― 株式配当や社会保障給付など、賃金以外の所得が家計の購買力を支える構図
 こうした所得環境に加え、株高による資産効果も消費を下支え。10月入り後も個人消費は底堅く推移
― 燃料高にもかかわらず、空港利用者数は前年並みの水準を維持し、小売売上高も高めの伸びを維持
― 年末商戦も前年を上回る伸びが予想されている

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大
（注） 食品は、コメと外食を除く食料価格。水道料金はその他財に該当。サービスは一般
サービスと公共サービスの合計（家賃を除く）
（出所） 総務省「消費者物価指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（注） 二人以上世帯、季節調整値。シャドー部分は景気後退局面
（出所） 内閣府「消費動向調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金
融政策は有効
 信用スプレッドの拡大などは「市場参加
者が、どのような投資を行いたいか、資
本配分を選別している」ことの表れ
AI投資
 「経済全体の生産性は、ここ2、3年ほど
上向いて」おり、高い経済成⾧率によっ
てインフレ圧力が生まれ、利上げが必要
な状況ではないとの認識
生産性
11月16日に生産性・雇用の公聴会。タスク
フォース報告は年末が目安（一部は遅延の
可能性）。バランスシート提言は2027年1
～3月期の早期を想定
タスク
フォース

### Page 8

内容ポイント
 26年3Qの景気は引き続き拡大
 エネルギー価格と資金調達コストの上昇が今後の重しに景気
 エネルギー価格の見通しは9月の予想を上回っている
 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ
 物価の基調・賃金はおおむね安定。ただし、価格形成の初
期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく
物価
 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、
複合的な要因が金利を押し上げ。加えて、債務の多い加盟
国は固有の課題に直面
 市場の不当かつ無秩序な動向が金融政策伝達の妨げとな
る場合、対応手段は用意されている
金融
環境
 全会一致で金利据え置きも、利上げが適切かどうか議論し
た。「今利上げを実施してはどうか」との意見もあった
 今後の金融政策はデータに基づき、会合ごとに決定

政策

金利水準 運転資金・在庫

（「増加」－「減少」、％）
欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
金利水準の高さが
貸出需要を下押し

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVAT免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

中国：製造業利益は引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引。7～9月期の利益率は前期を下回る
 9月の製造業総利益は前年比＋14.4％と前月（同＋9.1％）から加速。引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引
― コンピューター・通信機器は同＋72.9％の増益。石油・石炭加工は単月赤字から一転し、同10倍超の伸び
― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速

業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し
～転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方を探る～
（2026年10月2日発刊）
調査部レポートのご紹介 PR
2026年10月作成
レポート
URL
レポート
二次元
コード
世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
In-line 3Q26 results
Co-Tech reported 3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y).
• GM was 26.7% (-70bps q/q, +620bps y/y), 20/150bps below MSe/Street,
which we attributed to higher copper pricing and electricity rates (link).
• OP came in at NT$757mn (+11% q/q, +125% y/y), 1% above MSe but 5% below
the Street, for OpM of 23.9% (-50bps q/q, +690bps y/y), 30bps above MSe
but 140bps below the Street.

• As a result, NP was NT$608mn (+12% q/q, +140% y/y), in line with MSe but
4% below the Street, for EPS of NT$2.41.
• Co-Tech’s board also approved up to NT$7bn of CBs to fund the company's
Malaysian expansion. Issue price will be set by auction. Conversion price is
TBD pending regulatory approval.
• Reiterate OW; PT NT$745.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13
Sep 2026)

Key Takeaways

Our supply chain checks also suggest a key competitor could announce

Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO)
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity
of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 11.5%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.

Risks to Downside

n Faster-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
All Ring Tech
Bright 2027 outlook with multiple drivers
Intact 2027 outlook
All Ring's 2027 outlook is robust, supported by three key drivers. 1) We expect TSMC
and ASE to continue accelerating CoWoS capacity expansion, with industry capacity
reaching ~270kwpm by end-2027E, up from ~160kwpm by end-2026. We estimate
TSMC and ASE’s capacity could reach ~180kwpm and ~70kwpm, respectively, by
2027E. 2) Second, with Intel's EMIB-T progressing on track toward volume production

by early 2028, we expect related equipment demand to begin ramping in 2027,
providing a new growth driver for All Ring. 3) CPO’s fiber array unit (FAU) coupling tools
start contributing to All Ring's 2027 revenue, with demand from Nvidia and AMD.
More concrete CPO development in 2027-28
Our industry feedback suggests Nvidia is taking the lead to aggressively push CPO mass
production in 2027 for scale up switch solution. AMD is also engaging with key

equipment suppliers, in preparation for competing CPO solution. Competition among
the equipment vendors is fierce, but we believe All Ring is well positioned in FAU
coupling as a tier 1 supplier, given the superior execution in driving higher tool
throughput versus peers. Considering the meaningful tool demand in 2027-28, we
forecast All Ring to recognize ~100 tools for revenue in 2027E, contributing ~12% of All
Ring's sales next year. Potential upside to our current estimates could come from larger

TAM for faster capacity expansion, All Ring securing higher share / ASP through better
tool performance.
Lower Q3 gross margin due to mix
Q3 EPS came in below LSEG consensus by -10% as gross margin of 48.5% missed
consensus of 54.2%. Lower gross margin could be due to a higher mix of automation
products, which carries a lower margin vs corporate average. For Q4, considering lighter
seasonality and current visibility, we model sales to decline by -28% from Q3 with gross

margin of 53% and EPS of NT$7.57.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and target of $1700
Post Q3 results, we fine-tune 2026 EPS by -4% due to lower gross margin in the quarter,
though our estimates for 2027/28 are largely kept unchanged. Our 2027E/2028E EPS
are 3%/30% ahead of Consensus pre-earnings. We maintain our Buy rating and price
target of $1700 based on 27x average 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based supplier of automation equipment for the
semiconductor back-end packaging and passive component manufacturing industries. The
company's products include underfill dispensers, heatsink placement and automated optical
inspection (AOI) tools for advanced packaging equipment, particularly for heterogeneous

integration. Revenue is generated primarily from sales of semiconductor and electronic
manufacturing equipment and related automation solutions, with more than 90% of
revenue derived from Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value All Ring based on PE methodology.
Risks for All Ring and the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in

the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech

companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in All Ring
gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in All
Ring losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could

lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to All
Ring, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact All Ring’s
margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

기아 (000270)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.11.02  
잠시 쉬어갑니다

3Q26 Review 
기아는 3Q26P 매출액 31.6조원(+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.4% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회했다. 영업이익 증
감 요인은 환율 효과(+5,290억원), 물량 효과(+4,420억원), 믹스 효과(-4,650억원), 
인센티브/가격 효과(-7,270억원), 재료비 영향(-3,680억원), 기타(+4,130억원)이다. 
판매 성장에도 높아진 수익성 부담 
글로벌 도매 판매 84.7만대(+7.9% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 83.1만대
(+8.8%)를 기록했다. 미국 HEV와 서유럽 EV 판매가 각각 118%, 163% 증가하며 
글로벌 점유율은 4.0%까지 확대됐다. 다만, 판매 성장에도 원화 강세와 원자재 가격 
상승, EV 비중 확대에 따른 믹스 부담으로 수익성은 전분기 대비 하락했다. 유럽 내 
EV 판매가 늘어나면서 믹스가 악화되었으며, 전쟁으로 인한 원자재 상승으로 3분기 
재료비 부담은 전년 대비 약 3,700억 증가했다.  
연간 판매목표 335만대는 유지했으나, 4분기 수익성의 급격한 반등은 어려울 전망이
다. 향후 미국 텔루라이드·스포티지 HEV 공급 확대와 유럽 EV 판매 증가에 따른 고정
비 흡수가 수익성 회복의 핵심이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 155,000원으로 하향 
기아에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하고, 실적 추정치 하향을 반영해 목표주가를 
155,000원으로 하향한다. 재료비 부담, 환율 효과 축소, 예상보다 큰 믹스 악화로 단
기 이익 눈높이 조정은 불가피하다. 다만 산업수요 둔화에도 주요 시장에서 점유율을 
확대되고 있다는 점은 긍정적이다. 현재 배당 수익률은 7% 수준으로, 상대적으로 높
은 배당 매력은 주가 하방을 지지할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000 원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500 원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면 이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%) 
ㆍSTRONG BUY(매수) 추천기준일 종가대비 +50%이상 3%

Document 277: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors. As we 
transition to mid-cycle for the equity markets, we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8-10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non-hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven-year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short-term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck 
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy. With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near-term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year. However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify.  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar stocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate? As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key 
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.

US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic

US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.

Fixed Income: Still attractive all-in yields; prefer

US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30-year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more

In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion within the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income-
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.

continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels, as we 
think the Mideast situation should de-escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

5 FactSet, as of October 27, 2026.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.
A) October Saw a Sharp Broadening in Market Leadership. B) Value Has Quietly Led Growth in 2026.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Low volatility masks a wide range of tail risks for 2027. AI stress,
another inﬂation shock, ﬁscal concerns, elections and
geopolitical escalation could all trigger sharp FX moves, while
ﬁnancial repression and a carry unwind add further asymmetric
risks

Chris Turner and Francesco Pesole
AI bust: expect unorthodox FX reactions
The resilience of the AI trade in equities has been tested of late, and
a more decisive unwind could become an overarching FX driver next
year. We think the impact of a major US tech selloﬀ on currencies
would depend on how far it spreads to broader US and global risk
assets, whether it heavily threatens ﬁnancial stability, and how it
impacts the inﬂation backdrop. Crucially, the initial FX reaction may
look very diﬀerent from the medium-term move.
The dollar still retains decent safe-haven appeal by our estimates,
having recovered from the 2025 “sell America” period. Yet episodes
in 2026 where AI concerns hit tech stocks without materially

would continue to weigh moderately on the dollar next year, with
low-yielding haven currencies such as the yen, Swiss franc and euro
beneﬁting the most.
The dynamics would be diﬀerent if tech losses triggered a broader
selloﬀ. In that scenario, we would expect an initial safe-haven boost
to the dollar, followed by a larger depreciation as the shock

deepens and the Federal Reserve response comes into focus. The
greater the threat to ﬁnancial stability, the stronger the case for the
Fed to provide liquidity and prevent a global ﬁnancial crisis-style
credit crunch, ultimately weighing on the dollar. Over the medium
term, we would expect JPY, CHF and EUR to outperform, while the
Australian dollar, New Zealand dollar and non-European EM
currencies would be hit harder, consistent with recent AI-driven

selloﬀs (see chart below).
There is, however, an important inﬂation caveat. A severe risk-oﬀ
shock would itself reduce inﬂation risks through potential job
losses, negative wealth eﬀects and tighter credit conditions. But if
inﬂation remains elevated, the Fed may be slower to ease, not least
to preserve the safe-haven role of the Treasury market. In that
scenario, we think the dollar would outperform for longer, while
European currencies would be less insulated than under a more

benign inﬂation backdrop.
Exposure to an AI bust among selected
currencies

### Page 3

Asian currencies have been excluded due to time-zone mismatch
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
A new 2022 for inﬂation
Our baseline assumes contained second-round eﬀects from the
energy shock, allowing major European central banks to cut rates
towards the end of 2027. Four risks could challenge that view.
Second-round eﬀects may simply take longer to emerge, as a
prolonged crisis and higher gas prices erode corporate buﬀers and
feed into food and services inﬂation. Tighter labour markets could

amplify the shock through stronger wage growth, while renewed
Middle East escalation could drive another sharp rise in energy
prices. Finally, greater ﬁscal support could cushion the hit to
growth, but at the cost of higher medium-term inﬂation pressures.
In such a scenario, we would expect broad dollar gains. Historically,
low-yielders have tended to suﬀer most when Fed tightening drives
global yields higher, but we would be wary of applying that

playbook mechanically in 2027. The relative central bank response
should be the key diﬀerentiator, with those that have tightened less
so far retaining more ammunition to defend their currencies.
We still see the Norwegian krone and Swedish krona as particularly
vulnerable given their low liquidity and high beta, while AUD could
be a standout loser given the Reserve Bank of Australia's limited
room to tighten further amid domestic vulnerabilities. EUR and JPY,

by contrast, could prove more resilient than in 2022, provided the

European Central Bank and Bank of Japan respond swiftly to

Current central bank rates vs latest peak
Source: ING, Macrobond
Fiscal takeover: contained, but
potentially explosive risk
We’ve all seen the French bond sell-oﬀ unfold over recent
months. The heart of the story is one of budget credibility. If
investors suspect that a government will not or cannot amend a
worrying budget trajectory, then they will demand higher

coupons or, in extremis, as in Greece 2010, refuse to buy
government debt. France is no Greece and our base case
assumes that some modest budget consolidation can keep
French spreads contained in 2027. Even if French politicians fail
to address investor concerns, the ECB’s Transmission Protection
Instrument (TPI) backstop can prevent broader contagion.
What about the US? One area of concern is that near 60% of US
Treasury issuance is in the form of short-term bill issuance, as

well as its large reﬁnancing requirements. What happens if
inﬂation spikes and the US Treasury has to pay ever-higher
reﬁnancing rates? Equally, what happens if White House policy
again upends the dollar-US Treasury safe-haven correlation as
was the case in March/April 2025? As we saw at the time, the
breakdown in these correlations can trigger some of the most

extreme dollar selling. And the fact that investors are again
running low FX hedge ratios on US assets warns of a similar
setup to early 2025.
Debt ﬁnancing overview
Source: IMF, ING
Watch the EU election calendar!
2027 will be a busy year for eurozone political risk, with markets
focused primarily on ﬁscal implications and the threat of EU
political fragmentation. Expect any negative EUR impact to make
the Swiss franc a standout outperformer, thanks to its European

safe-haven role.
French elections combine both ﬁscal and EU-wide risks and are
likely to matter most. The biggest downside for the euro would
come from a surprise victory by Jean-Luc Mélenchon in the April-
May presidential election or a lack of a clear majority in potential
parliamentary elections over the summer. To a lesser extent, a
Marine Le Pen victory could also weigh on sentiment if early signals
point to a more confrontational approach towards the EU than

already expected.
In Germany, several state elections will be watched for signs of
greater government instability or further gains by the Eurosceptic
Alternative for Germany (AfD). Markets will focus particularly on the
implications for Germany’s reform agenda, and by extension the
growth outlook.

Italian elections rank third among our political risks. Although
scheduled by December, a May-June vote looks increasingly likely.
Prime Minister Giorgia Meloni remains favoured to secure another
mandate, potentially backed by a broader coalition. Italian asset
resilience has relied on Meloni’s ﬁscal discipline, so a surprise
victory by the centre-left bloc would, in our view, pose material
downside risks to BTPs and the euro.
A busy election calendar for next year

Source: ING, average of pollsters
Debasement trade strikes back
2025 was the year of dollar debasement, when fears over a
captured Fed led to pressure on the dollar against the Swiss franc,
while bitcoin and gold went bid. We had a ﬂash of that trade again
this August, when Scott Bessent’s extension of longer-dated US
Treasury buybacks unnerved the bond market and USD/CHF was hit
hard.
Were US Treasury yields to rise sharply next year, the theme of

‘ﬁnancial repression’ could re-emerge. Here the US Treasury could
try to contain Treasury yields by providing regulatory incentives to
banks to hold more Treasuries or put pressure on the Fed to
intervene. Any suggestion that the Treasury would not be allowing

USD is an efﬁcient safe-haven, but things
can change
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
The end of the carry trade?
Popular carry trade target currencies through 2026 have again
been the Turkish lira and the Nigerian naira. On the former, Turkish
presidential and parliamentary elections must be held by May
2028 and present some risk that the Central Bank of Turkey may

opt to change its policy of a controlled depreciation in the lira. That
would come as a big shock to the market. Nigeria looks slightly
better positioned after recent reforms to fuel subsidies and in the
energy sector.
When looking at the popular funding currencies of the yen, we
doubt slightly more aggressive BoJ tightening would be enough to
trigger a big short squeeze in the yen. And speculators are actually
running net long yen positions today as opposed to the large short

yen positions held in summer 2024 – the last time we had a major
adjustment in yen positioning.
The biggest driver of a carry trade unwind would probably come
through a ﬁnancial shock. Were any unwind in the AI trade to
uncover stresses or imbalances in, for example, the private credit
area and lead to ﬁnancial distress, volatility would surge and value-

Targeting Cookies

### Page 8

at-risk considerations could prompt indiscriminate deleveraging.
We haven’t really seen much ﬁnancial distress since the Silicon
Valley Bank crisis of 2023.
A big carry trade unwind would be yen and Swiss franc positive,
while negative for the high-yielding activity currencies.
Geopolitical blowout: more than one
front to watch
There are three major fronts to watch in terms of geopolitical tail
risks next year. All of these would see the dollar as the big

outperformer.
A spillover of Russia’s war beyond Ukraine, or a material escalation
in cyber and hybrid attacks on European countries, would hit
European currencies the hardest. CEE currencies and the
Scandinavian bloc would be particularly exposed given their
geographical proximity and sensitivity to regional security risks.
The Swiss franc and yen would join the dollar in a big rally.
A broader Middle East conﬂict drawing in additional regional or

external powers would mainly hit FX through higher energy prices.
Net energy importers would suﬀer most, particularly currencies
where central banks have limited scope for additional tightening to
oﬀset the inﬂationary shock. Here, the dollar would be a clear
winner, as other low-yielders would suﬀer from the energy shock.
A sharp escalation in geopolitical tensions in East Asia would put
Asian currencies under heavy pressure, with AUD and NZD also

vulnerable as liquid proxies. European currencies would be relatively
more insulated from the initial shock. Regional risks could also
include US-China trade and technology tensions.
Are geopolitical risks the new normal?

### Page 1

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

2026 /11/10

1

前日收盤價                              76.6元

許哲豪 
Jeff.syu@ctbcsis.com

GIS-KY(6456 TT)       法說/座談摘要 
雷射封裝業務進展順利，2Q27有望顯著放量 
⚫ 公司積極轉型自傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學 
⚫ 雷射封測試量產進行中，預期年底出貨，2027將有顯著貢獻 
結論：GIS-KY 3Q26營收有起色，但仍受終端市場需求不振及客戶端記
憶體缺貨的影響，但整體財務結構與淨現金部位仍穩健。公司目前正積
極推動業務轉型，從傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學領域，並已擴大資本
支出佈局光通訊雷射光源封測與 AR/VR等新產品，預計這些新事業將於
2027至 2028年成為公司顯著的營收成長動能。

3Q26財務數據 
⚫ 營收 144億元(QoQ+37.6%，YoY-10.5%)，主要受到終端市場需求
不振及客戶端記憶體缺貨的影響。 
⚫ 毛利率 4.02%(QoQ+0.68ppts，YoY-2.46ppts) 
⚫ 營業費用 12.11億元，前三季單季皆維持在 11.2億元左右的水準。
因公司處於轉型期，於 R&D的投資相對較多。 
⚫ 營業利益-6.3億元 
⚫ 稅前淨利-6.69億元 
⚫ 單季 EPS -3.15元 
⚫ 稅後淨利-7.25億元 
⚫ 單季 EPS -2.17元。 
產品營收占比 
⚫ 3Q26：平板佔 40%、筆電佔 28%、指紋辨識模組佔 13%、其他佔
19%。 
⚫ 1Q-3Q26：平板佔 32%、筆電佔 21%、指紋辨識模組佔 9%、其他
佔 38%。 
法說會內容摘要 
1.  公司中長期轉型策略：半導體光學(Semi Optics) 
• 光學元件：主要應用於 AR/VR眼鏡、Waveguide及抬頭顯示器
（HUD），涵蓋奈米壓印、蝕刻及鍍膜等製程，目前擁有多個國際大廠
專案。預期 AR/VR軟硬體需至 2027至 2028年才會成熟發酵。 
• 雷射封裝：為近期資本支出重點方向，主力產品為 CW Laser CoC
以及未來的 VCSEL VOD。

### Page 2

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

2026 /11/10

2

• 光通訊元件：發展被動光學元件，不排除整合 FAU、Metalens及上下游模零組件整合。 
• Micro OLED / Micro LED：Micro OLED在顯示器應用發展符合預期，並積極研發 Micro 
LED在光通訊領域的應用。

Q&A內容摘要 
1. Q：新世代半導體光學產品的資本支出目前的廠區規劃及進度為何？後續大客戶的營
收比重是否會下降？  
A：公告約 80億元的資本支出中，雷射封裝相關預計佔 40至 50億元，約三分之一投入台
灣廠區，其餘投入海外越南新廠。剩下的資本支出將用於 Micro LED、AR/VR波導及大客
戶新產品（廠區分散於大陸及台灣）。與大陸大客戶合作近 20年互信基礎深厚，只要有觸
控與顯示的新產品皆會參與，大客戶仍是重要客戶，但隨新業務逐步發酵，其營收佔比的
確會漸漸下降。

2. Q：10月份營收跳升破百億元的原因？這是一次性的嗎？這是否會佔用過多營運資
金？  
A：10月營收達 130億元，主因為客戶委託進行手機關鍵零組件的加工與精密對位貼合，
這是相對長期的專案，並非一次性。營收跳升主力來自高單價材料，若純看加工費，實際
營收佔比並不高。材料確實會佔用較大營運資金，但因週期短，銀行額度支持無虞；先前
提出的增資案主要是為支撐長期營運資金及較大資本支出。

3. Q：雷射封裝產線何時建好並貢獻營收？GIS在此領域相對於現有供應商的優勢在
哪？  
A：產線今年 2Q26已陸續建置，4Q26客戶原材料供應及認證趨於成熟，量產預計在 11月
或 12月從小量往上衝。GIS的優勢在於：(1)長期與國際大廠合作累積的製造管理能力。(2)
擁有充足現金流與財務實力，能支撐數十億的資本支出。

### Page 3

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

2026 /11/10

3 
4. Q：雷射出貨的規格？今明兩年的資本支出預估？  
A：目前專注於大於 300mW、400mW的高功率雷射（不涉獵傳統 100mW），主要應用於
ELSFP等 200G以上高階領域，材料主力為磷化銦，同時也開發 CW Laser及 VCSEL封裝。
今年已核准資本支出約 80億元，明年資本支出將視新產品狀況及客戶需求而定，預計今年
底或明年初會較明確。

5. Q：明年筆電與平板的記憶體缺貨狀況會好轉嗎？  
A：據業界交流，因新產能開出尚需時間，記憶體缺貨狀況到 2027年底前改善幅度可能有
限，許多人預估缺貨可能持續到 2028年。

6. Q：FAU的佈局？與集團內的訊芯有無合作？  
A：集團內公司皆獨立營運，訊芯比較接近光模塊加工。GIS初期不會聚焦整套 FAU模組
組裝，而是先從 passive component（如 Fiber holder、V型槽、透鏡陣列、超透鏡）做起，
未來若元件做得不錯，才會考慮後段的模組。

7. Q：大客戶即將推出搭載 OLED及觸控功能的筆電，貴司是否有參與？ A：不便針對
單一客戶產品發表評論。但只要是分離式的觸控模組（非內嵌式 In-cell Touch），公司都會
積極爭取並參與。

8. Q：雷射封裝做到 300mW以上，在技術或設備上有何門檻與優勢？ A：100mW提升
到 300mW或 400mW，封裝的製程流程基本相同，但測試項目及設備必須大幅更新，這也
是新資本支出的重點。基於過去在顯示器背光模組及指紋辨識貼合所累積的光學製程經
驗，跨入此領域對公司來說並非太難。

### Page 4

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

2026 /11/10

4 
9. Q：設備交期似乎很長，何時能滿產？ 
A：固晶機、打線機是泛用的，依照現有機台去挪動不難，主要是測試設備（如測試設備
外掛的電流計、頻譜分析儀）交期確實較長，預計 2Q27季底到 3Q27初產能可完全開出。
2Q27量產時預計會有一到兩家客戶。

10. Q：滿產能時的營收貢獻預估？何時能回收資本支出？  
A：雷射封裝商業模式以加工費為主，營收絕對金額佔比看似較小，但扣除材料費後，純
加工毛利率遠高於傳統觸控顯示業務。董事會對資本支出有嚴格的財務紀律，原則上必須
在少數幾年內完成回收。

11. Q：到 2H27可能 300mW以上的雷射市場變成熟，公司屆時產能到位可以拿下多少市
佔? 
A：公司有接觸多位 high power的客戶，每個客戶需求都很可觀，即便現在因為選舉政治
議題等潛在變數可能改變 AI的需求，但以整個產業來看，公司建置的產能僅占需求面的
一部分比例，仍有很大的增加產能的空間。

12. Q：與富采的 Micro LED及光通訊發展有何競爭或關聯？  
A：富采沒有做到封裝，GIS做封裝，雙方可能為上下游關係。富采 Micro LED偏向 TV巨
量轉移應用，GIS則聚焦 AR投影機光機應用，兩者 Micro LED大小差異很大。公司也與
客戶在探討 Micro LED或 Micro VCSEL在光通訊領域的潛力，均處於樣品開發階段。另外
公司今年 4Q26或 1Q27 AR projector可能會有小量出貨。

13. Q：光通訊相關的研發專案進度？以及信賴性測試（如 5000小時）的狀況？FAU會朝
模組發展嗎？  
A：不便針對單一客戶及具體的信賴性測試時數評論。關於 FAU，初期聚焦於光學元件（如
Fiber holder、波導、V型槽、超透鏡）。公司已具備 Metalens自行設計及製造的能力，因
其製程與目前開發的 AR眼鏡波導製程十分相近。

### Page 1

Flash Insights

2026年 11月 9日

1/3 
GPIF、運用姿勢に変化なし 
～2026年 9月末の運用実績速報、国内債券の比率引き上げ観測は後退か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

1. 2026年 7～9月の収益率はマイナス 1.65％ 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）は 2026年 11月6日、同年 7～9月の運用実績を公表
した。期間収益率はマイナス 1.65％だった。7～9月は国内外の長期金利が上昇し、債券価格が下
落した。加えて円高も進み、外国資産の円換算額を押し下げた。資産別の収益率は国内債券がマ
イナス 1.80％、外国債券がマイナス 6.52％、国内株式がプラス 3.71％、外国株式がマイナス
1.86％となった。9月末の運用資産額は314兆3,876億円と 6月末から約 3.4兆円減少した。 
GPIFを巡っては、片山さつき財務・金融相が 7月に「日本の金融資産にさらなる投資をしてい
ただくという方向で後押しをする方策を追求したい」と発言し、ポートフォリオに変化があるか
注目が集まっていた。長期金利が上昇していたこともあり、特に国内債への関心が高かった。 
結論から言うと、国内債比率は基本ポートフォリオの 25％に極めて近く、運用姿勢の変化を読
み取ることは難しい。国内債の比率を引き上げるのではないかという観測は一旦後退するだろ
う。

2. 国内債券比率は 25％近辺を維持  
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは国内外の株式と債券をそれぞれ 25％とし、国内債券には±6ポイ
ントの乖離許容幅が設定されている。GPIFの公表資料によると、年金積立金全体に占める国内債
比率は 2026年9月末時点で 26.49％だった。これには年金特別会計で管理する積立金、約 5.4兆
円が含まれている。 
この積立金を国内債券と年金積立金全体から除き、GPIFの運用資産ベースで計算すると、9月末
の国内債券比率は25.23％となる。6月末の 24.98％から 0.25ポイント上昇したが、基本ポートフ
ォリオの25％をわずかに上回る水準だ（資料1）。 過去の実績と比較しても、今回の比率は突出し
ていない。2020～2025年度の 9月末平均は 25.05％で、2026年9月末はこれを0.18ポイント上回
る程度だ。

### Page 2

2/3 
資料 1 GPIFの運用資産構成比率（推計、2026年 6月末・9月末）

（注）年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いたGPIF運用資産ベース。 
（出所）GPIF「2026年度第 1四半期運用状況（速報）」、「同第 2四半期運用状況（速報）」より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成。

3. 国内債券の買越額は推計 1.4兆円  
次に、7～9月の国内債券への買越額を推計する。6月末と 9月末の国内債券残高の変化から、
期間中の運用損益による増減を除いて推計した。 
年金特別会計で管理する積立金を除いた国内債券残高は、6月末の 79兆3,840億円から 9月末
の79兆3,206億円へ、634億円減少した。一方、7～9月の国内債券の運用損益はマイナス 1兆
4,452億円だった。この差額から、国内債券への買越額は約 1.4兆円と推計される（注 1）。4～6
月の約 5.7兆円と比較すると、国内債券への資金配分額は減少した（注2）。 
同じ方法で他の資産への資金配分を試算すると、外国債券は約 3.3兆円の買い越しとなった。
一方、国内株式は約2.0兆円、外国株式は約 0.8兆円の売り越しとなった。

4. 国内債券の運用姿勢に変化はみられず  
2026年7～9月の運用実績をみる限り、GPIFが国内債券の乖離許容幅を積極的に活用し、基本ポ
ートフォリオの25％を上回る水準へと比率を引き上げようとする動きは確認できなかった。 
国内債券は買い越しているとみられるものの、外国債券には国内債券を上回る資金が配分され
ている。4資産の比率はいずれも25％近い。基本ポートフォリオに近づけて運用する従来の姿勢
が維持されていると考えられる。 
以 上

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
【注釈】
1) 買越額は国内債券残高の変化から運用損益を控除した推計値。国内債券には為替ヘッジ付き外
国債券や円建て短期資産が含まれるため、日本国債の純購入額とは必ずしも一致しない。
2) 2026年7～9月は、GPIFの運用資産額の増減から運用収益額を差し引くと、約 1.9兆円の資金
流入があった計算になる。4～6月はほぼゼロだった。こうした資金流入の違いも、国内債券の
買越額を比較する際には考慮する必要がある。

 【粤开宏观】10 月出口延续高增：AI
拉动近四成增长，明年芯片涨价效应
或减弱 
2026 年 11 月 10 日

近期报告  
《【粤开宏观】美股大跌：有哪些原因？对
A 股有何影响？》2026-11-06 
《【粤开宏观】美国中期选举如何影响国内
政治、对华政策及资本市场？》2026-11-04 
《【粤开宏观】中国贸易顺差与外部失衡之
辩：六个关键问题》2026-10-27 
《【粤开宏观】积极因素持续积累，资本市
场稳健发展基础不断夯实》2026-10-23 
《【粤开宏观】全球 K 型分化的三大驱动：
AI、利率与债务》2026-10-20

导读 
11 月 10 日，海关总署公布进出口数据。2026 年 10 月，按美元计价，中国出
口 4123.3 亿美元，同比增长 35.2%，较上月小幅下降 1.1 个百分点；进口 3070.5
亿美元，同比增长 41.6%，较上月加快 10.6 个百分点。出口延续高增长，AI
产业链相关产品贡献突出，其中价格上涨的拉动作用尤为明显；进口增速明
显加快，主要受芯片、能源及部分原材料价格上涨推动。 
1、10 月出口延续高增，AI 产业链支撑突出，汽车及劳动密集型商品出口增
速有所放缓。分商品看，一是全球 AI 算力需求旺盛，叠加存储等芯片价格上
涨，推动相关产品出口高增。10 月，集成电路和自动数据处理设备出口同比
分别增长 137.1%和 114.8%，增速较上月分别提高 1.1 和 21.8 个百分点，合计
拉动整体出口增长 13.6 个百分点，贡献了近四成的出口增幅。其中，集成电
路出口均价同比上涨 143.0%，出口数量下降 2.4%，价格上涨是出口金额高增
的主要原因。二是汽车出口增速明显放缓，主要受前期对欧盟“抢出口”效
应减弱影响。受中欧混动汽车贸易安排预期影响，部分车企为应对政策调整
的不确定性，在政策落地前集中出货，导致 10 月汽车出口增速由上月的 62.0%
降至 37.6%。三是劳动密集型商品出口增速有所放缓。10 月，服装、鞋靴、
箱包、玩具和家具出口同比均较上月回落，但仍保持两位数增长。 
分市场看，对韩国、中国台湾等市场出口较快增长，主要受全球 AI 产业链需
求扩张带动；对拉美、非洲出口增速回升，新兴市场基础设施建设和工业化
需求继续提供支撑；对美国、东盟和欧盟出口增速有所回落，但仍保持较快
增长。 
2、10 月进口增速创年内新高，AI 产业链相关产品进口高增与大宗商品涨价
共同推动。2026 年二季度以来，进口增长呈现明显的“价强量弱”特征，价
格上涨对进口金额的拉动较为突出。一是全球 AI 产业链景气延续，相关产品
进口保持高增长。10 月，集成电路和自动数据处理设备进口金额同比分别增
长 78.3%和 261.0%。其中，集成电路进口均价同比上涨 78.2%，进口数量仅
增长 0.1%，表明全球 AI 算力需求旺盛带动高端芯片价格上涨，是相关产品
进口金额高增的重要原因。二是地缘政治冲突加剧能源供给扰动，国际能源
价格大幅上涨，显著推高进口金额。10 月，布伦特原油现货均价同比上涨
92.3%，中国原油、煤及褐煤、天然气进口均价分别上涨 62.6%、69.4%和 46.6%，
而进口数量分别下降 13.3%、5.5%和增长 2.4%，价格上涨是能源进口金额增
长的主要驱动力。三是部分上游原材料价格上涨，同样对进口金额形成支撑。
10 月，铜矿砂及其精矿进口金额同比增长 23.1%，其中进口均价上涨 39.2%，
进口数量下降 11.6%。 
3、展望 2027 年，全球 AI 需求仍有较强支撑，但芯片价格涨幅收窄可能推动
中国出口增速放缓。当前，全球 AI 基础设施投资仍处于扩张阶段，算力建设
及相关设备需求有望延续。尽管美国部分地区数据中心建设监管趋严，可能
影响部分项目的审批进度和区域布局，但对 2027 年既定建设计划的影响相对

### Page 2

宏观深度 
请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  2 / 11 
有限，难以改变全球 AI 投资持续扩张的总体趋势。 
相比需求收缩，存储芯片价格涨幅收窄对明年中国 AI 产业链出口增长的影响
可能更为直接。2026 年相关商品出口高增，既受益于全球 AI 需求扩张，也
受到芯片价格大幅上涨的推动。展望 2027 年，随着新增产能逐步释放，存储
价格走势可能分化，叠加今年价格大幅上涨形成的高基数，价格对相关商品
出口金额增长的拉动可能减弱。总体来看，明年 AI 产业链出口仍有较强需求
支撑，但增速可能较今年放缓，叠加整体出口高基数及外部贸易摩擦等因素，
预计 2027 年中国整体货物出口同比增长 8%—10%。

一、出口延续高增，AI 产业链仍是核心支撑 
10 月中国出口增速小幅回落，但仍延续强劲增长态势。2026 年 10 月，中国出口同
比增长 35.2%，增速较上月回落 1.1 个百分点，仍创近二十年同期新高。增速边际放缓，
部分源于前期汽车企业在中欧贸易磋商前集中出货，提前释放部分出口需求，随着“抢
出口”效应减弱，相关商品出口增速有所回落。全球制造业景气度维持较高水平，叠加
AI 产业链需求旺盛，共同支撑中国出口韧性。10 月全球制造业 PMI 为 52.6%，虽较上月
回落 0.4 个百分点，但已连续 15 个月处于扩张区间，表明外需仍较旺盛。同时，AI 产业
链景气延续，相关电子产品出口保持较快增长，中国出口仍具有较强支撑。 
图表1：2026 年 10 月，进出口延续高位增长

资料来源：Wind、粤开证券研究院 
分市场看，10 月中国对主要贸易伙伴出口普遍保持较快增长，其中对韩国、拉美和
非洲出口增速较上月进一步提高。10 月，中国对韩国、拉美和非洲出口分别同比增长
92.4%、32.1%和 19.5%，分别较上月提高 24.3、7.4 和 3.4 个百分点；对东盟、美国、欧
盟出口分别同比增长 46.3%、32.9%和 24.4%，分别较上月回落 5.9、6.6 和 0.9 个百分点。 
对韩国、中国台湾等市场出口较快增长，主要受益于全球 AI 需求扩张带动的产业
链贸易增长。2026 年 1—9 月，中国对韩国、中国台湾出口的 AI 产业链相关商品分别同
比增长 112.9%和 58.8%，对两地出口增长的贡献率均在八成左右。中国台湾和韩国分别
在先进芯片制造、计算硬件和存储芯片等环节具有突出优势，中国则广泛参与电子组件、
设备及配套产品供应。随着全球 AI 需求快速扩张，产业链上下游跨境协作更加紧密，带
动中国对韩国、中国台湾相关商品出口快速增长。 
对非洲、拉美等新兴市场出口增速回升，基础设施建设和工业化需求成为 AI 之外
支撑中国装备制造产品出口增长的重要力量。非洲、拉美等地区正处于基础设施完善和
工业化发展的重要阶段，对交通运输、工程建设和工业生产设备的需求持续释放。中国
汽车、叉车、工程机械等产品具有较强的性价比和供应链优势，非洲、拉美已成为相关
商品的重要出口市场，为中国出口增长提供了新的支撑。 
对东盟、美国和欧盟出口增速小幅回落，主要是 9 月高增长后的正常回归，并不意
味着出口需求明显转弱。9 月中国对上述市场出口规模均创年内新高，部分源于国庆假
期前集中出货及中欧贸易磋商前的“抢出口”效应，提前释放了部分出口需求。随着短

期因素消退，10 月出口增速边际回落，但整体仍保持较快增长，出口韧性依然较强。 
图表2：2026 年 10 月，中国对主要经济体出口均维持较快增长

资料来源：海关总署、粤开证券研究院 
分商品看，AI 产业链相关产品出口延续高增，汽车和劳动密集型商品出口增速边际
放缓。 
其一，AI 产业链仍是出口增长的核心驱动力，价格上涨是重要支撑。10 月，集成
电路和自动数据处理设备出口同比分别增长 137.1%和 114.8%，增速较上月分别提高 1.1
和 21.8 个百分点，合计拉动当月出口增长 13.6 个百分点。从量价关系看，出口金额高增
主要由价格上涨驱动，数量表现相对偏弱。其中，集成电路出口均价同比上涨 143.0%，
出口数量下降 2.4%；手机出口金额同比增长 13.2%，出口均价上涨 38.4%，出口数量下
降 18.2%。当前 AI 产业链需求旺盛及相关产品价格上涨持续推升出口金额，但价格与数
量走势分化，出口增长对价格因素的依赖仍然较高。 
其二，劳动密集型商品出口增速高位回落，但修复趋势延续。10 月，服装、鞋靴、
箱包、玩具和家具出口同比分别增长 19.5%、14.2%、17.9%、27.5%和 13.2%，增速较上
月分别回落 4.3、6.0、4.7、13.3 和 8.8 个百分点，但仍保持两位数增长。1—10 月，上述
五类商品出口累计同比增长 4.5%，增速较 1—9 月提高 1.3 个百分点，表明劳动密集型商
品出口整体仍处于修复通道。 
其三，汽车出口增速高位回落，主要受前期“抢出口”效应减弱及部分需求提前释
放影响。10 月，汽车（包括底盘）出口金额同比增长 37.6%，增速较上月回落 24.4 个百
分点。此前，受中欧混动汽车贸易安排预期影响，部分企业在政策落地前集中出货，9
月中国对欧盟出口的汽车及其零附件同比增长 82.6%，提前释放了部分后续出口需求。

二、进口增速创年内新高，AI 相关产品与能源涨价共同推动 
10 月进口增速创年内新高，价格上涨是重要推动因素。2026 年 10 月，中国进口同
比增长 41.6%，增速较上月大幅提高 10.6 个百分点。从量价关系看，2026 年二季度以来，
进口增长呈现明显的“价强量弱”特征。9 月进口价格指数为 128.7，数量指数为 96.8，
分别对应进口价格同比上涨 28.7%、数量同比下降 3.2%，表明此前进口金额高增主要由
价格因素驱动。 
其一，AI 相关商品及上游原材料进口保持高增长，价格上涨贡献突出。10 月，铜
矿砂及其精矿、集成电路和自动数据处理设备进口金额同比分别增长 23.1%、78.3%和
261.0%。其中，铜矿砂及其精矿、集成电路进口均价分别同比上涨 39.2%和 78.2%，涨
幅较上月进一步扩大，而进口数量分别下降 11.6%和增长 0.1%。AI 算力建设需求旺盛，
带动芯片等关键硬件进口增长，相关产品价格上涨进一步推高进口金额。 
其二，能源产品进口增长主要由国际价格上涨驱动。地缘政治冲突加剧能源供给扰
动，推动国际能源价格大幅上涨。10 月布伦特原油现货均价达 125 美元/桶，同比增长
92.3%。10 月，中国原油、煤及褐煤、天然气进口金额同比分别增长41.0%、60.0%和50.2%，
进口均价分别上涨 62.6%、69.4%和 46.6%，而进口数量分别下降 13.3%、5.5%和增长 2.4%。
能源进口金额普遍较快增长，但实物进口量表现偏弱，表明进口增长更多来自价格上涨，
而非实际需求扩张。

三、2027 年中国 AI 出口：美国 AI 需求仍有支撑，价格支撑
可能减弱 
美国中期选举后，部分地方政府趋严的数据中心建设监管，引发市场对美国 AI 投资
需求及中国相关商品出口增长可持续性的担忧。当前，AI 产业链已成为中国出口增长的
重要引擎，而美国是全球 AI 算力建设的主要需求市场，地方监管趋严可能增加数据中心
项目落地的不确定性，进而影响全球 AI 硬件需求。我们认为，美国地方监管趋严可能影
响部分数据中心项目的审批进度和区域布局，但对 2027 年既定建设计划的冲击相对有限，
难以改变 AI 投资持续扩张的总体趋势。 
第一，美国地方监管收紧对 2027 年数据中心建设的直接影响相对有限，影响可能更
多在 2028 年及以后显现。地方政府的审批限制主要影响尚未开工的新建项目，已获批、
在建项目受影响相对较小。据研究机构 SemiAnalysis 统计，美国前 20 个州计划于 2027
年交付的 33.8 吉瓦数据中心容量中，约四分之三已经开工，相关项目建设进度相对确定。
相比之下，计划于 2028 年投产的项目仍有较多处于审批、供电工程和施工排期阶段，更
容易受到地方政策调整的影响。 
第二，美国地方监管收紧难以改变 AI 投资扩张的基本趋势。一方面，地方监管主
要回应居民对电价上涨、电力供应紧张及公共资源占用的担忧，政策重点在于规范数据
中心建设及成本分担，而非限制 AI 产业发展。另一方面，维护 AI 技术领先地位是美国
两党的重要共识，地方监管趋严与联邦政府支持 AI 产业发展的战略方向并不必然冲突。
未来，随着审批标准和成本分担机制逐步明确，部分合规项目有望恢复推进，新增投资
也可能向能源供应充裕、土地成本较低的地区转移。因此，地方监管可能改变数据中心
建设节奏和区域布局，但未必导致全美 AI 算力投资总量明显萎缩。

### Page 9

请务必阅读最后特别声明与免责条款 www.ykzq.com  9 / 11
但仍需警惕 AI 投资预期变化通过供应链“牛鞭效应”放大中国相关商品出口的波
动。全球 AI 硬件产业链分工较长，美国云厂商的算力投资需求需要经过芯片供应商、服
务器代工厂及零部件供应商等多个环节，才能最终转化为中国相关商品的出口订单。在
需求扩张阶段，各环节为保障交付、应对供应短缺，往往提前备货、增加安全库存，使
上游订单增长快于终端实际需求；而当云厂商推迟数据中心建设或下调资本开支预期时，
下游企业可能通过减少备货、推迟采购和消化库存等方式调整订单，导致上游供应商订
单降幅超过终端需求变化。因此，即使美国 AI 投资需求未出现趋势性下降，短期预期调
整也可能通过供应链库存周期放大中国 AI 相关商品出口的波动。
而相比需求收缩，存储价格涨幅收窄对 2027 年中国 AI 产业链出口增长的影响可能
更为直接。中国 AI 产业链出口增长主要由存储、计算等集成电路及搭载相关芯片的自动
数据处理设备带动，2026 年相关商品出口高增中，价格上涨贡献较大。以集成电路为例，

2026 年 1—10 月，出口均价同比上涨 111.1%，出口数量同比仅增长 3.2%。
2027 年存储价格走势可能分化，叠加 2026 年高基数影响，价格对出口增长的拉动
可能边际减弱。一方面随着新增产能逐步释放、部分终端需求承压，存储产品普遍大幅
涨价的局面可能有所缓解。据研究机构 TrendForce 预测，受 AI 服务器需求增长、HBM
产能挤占及新增产能释放滞后等因素影响，DRAM 供应仍将偏紧；NAND Flash 则呈现
企业级需求旺盛、消费级需求疲弱的分化特征，随着新增产能逐步释放，2027 年下半年
供需可能趋于宽松。另一方面，2026 年存储价格大幅上涨抬高了 2027 年的同比基数。
据 TrendForc 统计，2026 年第三、第四季度一般型 DRAM 合约价格环比分别上涨 13%—
18%和 10%—15%，涨幅较第一、二季度的 90%—95%和 58%—63%明显收窄。即使 2027
年部分存储产品价格继续维持高位，价格同比涨幅也可能收窄，对中国 AI 产业链出口金

额增长的拉动相应减弱。
总体来看，2027 年全球 AI 需求仍有较强支撑，中国 AI 产业链相关商品出口有望保
持增长，但增速可能较 2026 年高位回落，叠加整体出口高基数及外部贸易摩擦等因素，
预计中国整体出口增速回落至 8%—10%。

Document 502: 中东原油“全面夺回”亚洲市场，美国至亚洲油轮运价从高点跌61%

Source PDF: all report pdf/中东原油“全面夺回”亚洲市场，美国至亚洲油轮运价从高点跌61%.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

中东原油对亚洲出⼝回暖，亚洲炼油商采购重⼼转回传统供应来源，美国原油替代需求减
弱。美国墨西哥湾⾄亚洲 VLCC ⽇收益较 10 ⽉峰值跌 61% ，波斯湾⾄东亚仅跌 24% 。⽇
本、泰国等重启或扩⼤中东采购， 11 ⽉交付美国原油订单骤降约 50% 。
摘要：
中东原油流向亚洲正在回升，亚洲炼油商的采购重⼼逐步转回传统供应来源，美国原油的替代
性需求随之减弱。
11 ⽉ 13 ⽇，据彭博报道，美国墨西哥湾⾄亚洲航线的超⼤型原油运输船（ VLCC ）⽇收益较 10
⽉峰值下跌 61% ，⽽波斯湾⾄东亚航线的同类指标仅下跌 24% ，两条航线运价⾛势明显分化。
采购数据也印证了这⼀趋势。⽇本、泰国等亚洲炼油商正在重启或扩⼤中东原油采购。彭博此
前报道称，亚洲炼油商订购的 11 ⽉交付美国原油数量较此前骤降约 50% ，⾄ 2400 万⾄ 2600 万
桶。
据报道援引知情交易员，来⾃阿联酋、沙特阿拉伯和卡塔尔的原油流量均已显著扩⼤，其中包
括需要穿越霍尔⽊兹海峡运输的货物。随着中东产油国产量恢复、售价下调，亚洲原油进⼝格

局正逐步回归美伊冲突前的状态。
中东供应恢复，产油国下调售价争夺市场
今年 2 ⽉美伊冲突爆发初期，途经霍尔⽊兹海峡的油轮遭到袭击，波斯湾产油国原油出⼝⼤幅收
缩，亚洲买家被迫转向美国等替代供应来源。
随着局势趋于稳定，中东原油供应逐步恢复。沙特阿拉伯向欧佩克报告称，其 10 ⽉原油产量升
⾄近年来的⽉度⾼位之⼀。
与此同时，主要产油国也在通过降价争夺亚洲市场份额。沙特近期将⾯向亚洲买家的官⽅售价
下调⾄六年来低位；已于 4 ⽉退出欧佩克的阿联酋，也下调了 12 ⽉交付旗舰原油品种的价格溢
价。
荷兰国际集团（ ING Groep NV ）⼤宗商品策略主管 Warren Patterson 表⽰，亚洲很难摆脱对中
东这⼀最⼤原油供应来源的依赖，因为全球供需平衡中缺乏⾜够的额外供应，难以⽀持采购来
源的⼤规模转移。
亚洲买家回流中东，美国原油订单减少
⽇本炼油商已经开始增加中东原油采购。 Eneos Holdings 和 Cosmo Energy Holdings 近期均有
采购阿联酋原油的记录。

乐天证券经济研究所分析师 Yutaro Nishi 表⽰，只要⽇本炼油商仍能从中东获得原油，就很可能
继续采购。他指出，尽管各国情况有所不同，但亚洲仍将是中东原油的主要市场。
印度的采购结构也出现变化。根据 Kpler 数据，来⾃波斯湾的原油进⼝量已超过俄罗斯，重新成
为印度最⼤的原油进⼝来源。
